|  |  | RSK-01 | Supply chain | Which critical suppliers, materials, components, or services have supply-chain risk? | Critical-item assessment | Assumption | Drafted | Critical items: robotic actuators/end-effector (Aetheris Space Robotics), the radar subsystem (Northstar Orbital Technologies), and imaging/proximity sensors (Borealis Vision Systems). | Assumption; OBS | Naomi Fitzgerald (Lead Spacecraft Systems Engineer) | Medium | Long-lead supply risk. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  | RSK-02 | Delay response | What contingency plans, alternate suppliers, schedule margin, or advance purchases are assumed for supply-chain delays? | Mitigation and cost treatment | Assumption | Drafted | Schedule margin held in Phase C/D, an alternate sensor source qualified, and advance purchase of long-lead propulsion from Northstar. | Assumption | Samuel Boucher (Schedule and Cost Management Engineer) | Low | Margin and advance-buy cost. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  | RSK-03 | Foreign exchange and escalation | What inflation, escalation, exchange-rate, tariff, and commodity-price assumptions apply? | Rates, dates, and sources | Assumption | Drafted | CAD-base estimate; assume 2.5%/yr escalation, limited USD exposure on launch and some components, FY2024 economic base. | Costing GR&A (Part C) | Samuel Boucher (Schedule and Cost Management Engineer) | Medium | Escalation on multi-year build. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  | RSK-04 | Schedule constraints | What fixed dates, launch windows, partner milestones, seasonal constraints, or long-lead items drive the schedule? | Constraints and margins | Ground rule | Drafted | Launch window is fixed by planetary alignment for the Jupiter gravity assist (host orbiter, NASA-led); long-lead sampling-arm hardware drives the ORCA-side critical path. | Sched Dependencies (Part D §10) | Naomi Fitzgerald (Lead Spacecraft Systems Engineer) | Medium | Critical-path driver. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |

|  |  | SUS-01 | Disposal compliance | How will ORCA comply with post-mission disposal and planetary-protection guidelines? | Compliance approach | Ground rule | Drafted | Controlled disposal of the NEREID orbiter and its science payload complement to atmospheric re-entry within guideline timelines; compliant with COSPAR Planetary Protection Policy and NASA/ESA Enceladus contamination-control requirements, and Canadian direction. | COSPAR / NASA-ESA planetary-protection requirements; Part A §2.1 | Naomi Fitzgerald (Lead Spacecraft Systems Engineer) | High | Central mission objective; ## 11 - DDDD

| ` Table of Contents |  |
| --- | --- |
|  |  |
| 12 - Dispose, Divest, Descope, Delay (DDDD) |  |
| Why we we asking: |  |
| This section helps assess program risk, maturity, and readiness for Earned Value Management (EVM). It explores how you would respond to cost overruns, schedule delays, and end-of-life considerations. |  |
|  |  |
|  |  |
| Q1. COST OVERRUNS |  |
| If the cost exceeds the approved allocation, what mitigation strategies would you consider? ☐ Descope  ☐ Request additional funding  ☐ Delay   ☐ Cannibalize from the A-Base  ☐ Other (specify) | Selected: [x] Descope  [x] Delay  [x] Request additional funding. Primary response is to descope (reduce plume-pass count) before seeking new funds; cannibalizing the A-Base is a last resort. |
| If you have a Descope Plan, please describe it. | Yes. Tiered descope: (1) reduce plume-pass count from multi- to single-pass; (2) defer extended operations / disposal (Phase X); (3) descope onboard autonomy to ground-in-the-loop operations. Each ties to a KDP off-ramp. |
| If you would request more funding, would this be an A-Base pressure or a Fiscal Framework Ask ? | A Fiscal Framework Ask for material growth; minor pressures absorbed as an A-Base pressure within the CSA envelope. |
| If an A-Base pressure, which other investment would be most at risk of cannibalization? | If A-Base, the CSA space-robotics technology development line would be most at risk of cannibalization. |
| Do you have a Scope Management Strategy in place? | Yes - baseline is held under the CSA Configuration Control Board; a RID (Review Item Disposition) process runs at each gate; scope changes above the TB threshold escalate. |
| Who is your Systems Engineer, and what is their approach to RIDs and scope escalation? | Lead Spacecraft Systems Engineer: Naomi Fitzgerald. RIDs are dispositioned at each gate review; scope escalation is controlled via ECR to the CCB with a cost/schedule impact assessment. |
| How well is the scope defined? What level of uncertainty exists in the scope? | Scope is well defined for the ORCA instrument suite but carries moderate uncertainty in the sampling-arm and autonomy elements (TRL 6). Overall Mission Class B. |
| Do you have a vendor exit strategy, such as go/no-go milestones? | Yes - go/no-go at Gate 2 (down-select of the two Phase 0 concepts) and at each subsequent gate; option clauses allow an off-ramp before the Phase D commitment. |
| What thresholds would trigger a replanning or a rebaseline? | Rebaseline if cost variance exceeds +15% of the approved envelope, or schedule variance exceeds 6 months at a gate. |
|  |  |
| Q2. DELAYS |  |
| If the mission extends beyond nominal operations, do you have a Divestiture Plan?   Who would you divest to?   Are there any financial interests involved in the divestiture? | No divestiture - ORCA is a single-use CSA science payload. Extended operations run under Phase X until end-of-life, then controlled disposal. No third-party financial interest. |
| What would be the impacts of a significant launch delay? | A launch slip on the host orbiter (NASA-led) delays science-data return, extends CSA salary/O&M carrying cost, and may miss the Jupiter gravity-assist window entirely. Roughly $0.3-0.5M per month of standing-army cost (the Pessimistic scenario carries a 1-year slip). |
| Do you have contingency plans for schedule delays? | Yes - schedule margin is held in Phase C/D, an alternate sensor source is qualified, and long-lead sampling-arm hardware is advance-purchased. Three schedule scenarios (Nominal / Optimistic / Pessimistic) bound the risk. |
| What schedule delays would trigger a replanning or a rebaseline? | A slip greater than 6 months against the Scenario 1 baseline at any gate triggers replanning; greater than 12 months triggers a formal rebaseline. |
|  |  |
|  |  |
| Q3. END OF LIFE |  |
| Do you have a general idea of the Disposal Plan for Phase F? | Disposal is handled in Phase X (Phase F/G collapsed into Phase X for ORCA): controlled disposal of the NEREID orbiter and its science payload complement per the partner agency plan, compliant with COSPAR / NASA-ESA planetary-protection requirements. |
| Are you planning an Audit after launch? | Yes - a post-launch / close-out audit and a lessons-learned review are planned at the end of Phase X. |
| What are your plans for retiring the space segment after nominal operations?   Do you plan to divest any components? If so, how? And to whom? | The space segment is disposed of per the partner agency's end-of-mission plan, not retired for reuse; no components are divested. Ground assets (the MOC) revert to CSA sustaining use. |
| Do you plan to de-orbit the spacecraft or transfer it to a parking orbit? What is your strategy for managing space debris? | Controlled end-of-mission disposal per the partner agency's planetary-protection plan - never left in an uncontrolled state. The mission is planetary-protection compliant (controlled instrument operations with abort-to-safe). |
| What is the source of funds for a potential Phase E Extension? | A-Base O&M appropriation funds nominal Phase E; any extension (Phase X) is funded through a subsequent Fiscal Framework Ask or reprofiled A-Base. |
|  |  |

## 20 - Lessons Learned

| ` Table of Contents |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| 21 - Costing Lessons Learned |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| Why we we asking: |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| What previous lessons did we learn and apply here? | Applied lessons from prior CSA robotics/servicing costing: budget long-lead space-robotics items early, size autonomy software with a dedicated CER, and carry FX contingency on foreign launch/components. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| What new lessons did we learn by doing this? | New lessons are captured below - chiefly that outer-planet instrument missions lack mature cost analogues and that disposal-phase (Phase X) duration is a major cost sensitivity. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  | ID | Category | Title | Description of Driving Event | Lesson Learned | Corrective Action or Recommendation |
|  |  |  | LL-01 | Schedule | Long-lead sampling-arm hardware | Sampling-arm end-effector actuators (Aetheris) drove the Phase C/D critical path. | Long-lead sampling-arm items must be identified and ordered before CDR. | Add an advance-procurement line at PDR and carry schedule margin in Phase C. |
|  |  |  | LL-02 | Cost / RAM | Instrument autonomy software effort underestimated | Flight-software and autonomy scope grew beyond the RAM baseline. | Autonomous instrument-sequencing software is a major cost driver, not a minor line. | Size software with a dedicated CER and a 20% reserve; run agile with EVM. |
|  |  |  | LL-03 | Procurement | Sole-source sampling-arm supplier | Limited domestic competition for instrument accommodation (near-incumbent Aetheris). | Sole-source premiums must be budgeted and justified early. | Run an RFI in Phase 0; negotiate open-book pricing for the directed contract. |
|  |  |  | LL-04 | Estimating | Few outer-planet instrument-suite analogues | Outer-planet flagship instrument contributions lack mature parametric cost analogues. | Deep-space instrument missions need bottom-up estimates plus expert judgment. | Build a calibrated CER set and document analogy adjustments in Part B. |
|  |  |  | LL-05 | Schedule / Ops | Phase X duration uncertainty | Extended ops plus disposal (Phase X) has wide uncertainty. | Disposal-phase duration and cost are highly sensitive to extended-mission scope. | Model Phase X in all three schedule scenarios; tie ops cost to science-pass count. |
|  |  |  | LL-06 | Risk | FX exposure on instrument hardware | USD exposure on the sampling-arm and avionics procurements (Aetheris, Polarion). | Currency risk on foreign-sourced components can move the estimate materially. | Carry the FX assumption in Constants and hold FX contingency in the risk reserve. |
|  |  |  | LL-07 | Governance | IGMF gate re-mapping | Removing Phase F/G and adding Phase X required re-mapping the IGMF gates. | Non-standard phasing must be reconciled with IGMF gate nomenclature early. | Document the phase-to-gate mapping in Part D 8 before dating milestones. |
|  |  |  | LL-08 | Staffing | Scarce space-robotics talent | Recruitment of GNC/robotics specialists lagged the RAM plan. | Specialist availability constrains schedule as much as funding does. | Start recruitment and knowledge transfer in Phase B; budget training. |
